股價相對大盤走勢

全新 (2455 TT)  Buy

2H26 、 2027 年營收動能可期，評等為 Buy。  
投資建議 ：07/2026 全新營收 4.22 億元， MoM+29.48% ，創歷史
新高，主要係 AI data center 相關需求持續熱絡，且出貨表現亦
隨磷化銦基板料況改善而回歸常軌所致，且 2H26、2027 年營收
動能仍可期，故群益對全新的投資建議為 Buy 。  
07/2026 營收創新高： 2Q26 營收 9.65 億元， QoQ+0.63% ，其中，
微電子營收 7.04 億元， QoQ-1.89% ，與 1Q26 相去有限；光電子
產品營收 2.61 億元， QoQ+8.12% ，但 01~06/2026 僅 02/2026 受
農曆春節影響而僅 0.68 億元，其餘各月份光電子營收均為
0.86~0.88 億元，換言之，缺料問題仍影響光電子產品出貨表現。  
不過， 07/2026 營收 4.22 億元， MoM+29.48% ，創歷史新高，關
鍵在於磷化銦基板缺料狀況將隨中國政府 05/2026 、 07/2026 兩
度放行管制而改善 ， 帶動全新光電子產品營收增加至 1.69 億元 ，
MoM+93.74% ；至於微電子產品營收 2.53 億元，亦為近幾年相
對高水準。  
2H26 展望優於 1H26：雖然 AI data center 相關需求熱絡，然而，
01~06/2026 光電子產品月營收成長性仍受制於磷化銦基板缺
料。全新磷化銦基板過去多數由美國業者的中國廠供應，然而，
受到中國政府管制磷化銦基板出口數量影響， 4Q25 全新光電子
產品營收逐月減少，且全新來自該供應商的磷化銦基板庫存
12/2025 已陸續用罄， 01~06/2026 磷化銦基板主要由第 2、 3 供
應商供應。  
所幸，中國政府 05/2026 月底批准 2026 年第 1 批磷化銦基板出
口， 07/2026 則再通過第 2 批，且總數量大於第 1 批，第 1、 2
批合計數量更已超過 2025 年總數量，此外，全新 06/2026 已陸
續接獲第 1 批獲准出口的磷化銦基板並用以生產光電子產品，
2H26 展望優於 1H26 。

### Page 2

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 9 月 3 日

2 群益投顧 
  企業 ESG 資訊揭露：  
就企業溫室氣體排放量減量目標而言，由於全新專注於製造、銷售及研發化
合物半導體磊晶片，溫室氣體排放量多寡，主要和能源耗用、產能及產品組
成有關。全新乃就單位產品的能源耗用與溫室氣體排放量，設定目標為每年
減少 5%，並將視產能擴充與產品變化情形，賡續追蹤與調整設定與目標。
至於企業產品或服務帶給客戶或消費者之減碳效果的部份，全新為客戶代
工生產的產品多數運用於通訊等電子產品，非屬終端消費性產品；全新除協
助客戶設計低耗能、高效率產品，以降低產品生命週期內的資源耗用外，亦
持續進行生產製造過程的節能與減廢，以降低對環境所造成的衝擊，減少單
位產品所消耗的資源與污染物，為客戶及社會創造減碳環保效益。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 3

Hon Hai (2317.TW; Buy, on CL): Jul rev in line at +54% YoY; AI server and smartphones to support 3Q 
growth

Hon Hai’s Jul revenues were +54% YoY/ +15% MoM to NT$947m, or 2% higher than our 
estimates, overall in line. From a MoM perspective, Consumer Electronics & 
Components delivered significant growth MoM, benefitting from the pull-in 
momentum, followed by Cloud and Networking & Computing which achieved strong 
growth MoM. From a YoY perspective, all segments across Consumer Electronics, 
Components, Cloud and Networking & Computing delivered strong growth YoY, and 
management attributed this to the strong pull-in momentum and higher selling prices of 
Consumer Electronics and Computing products. We expect 3Q26E revenues to be +48% 
YoY / +22% QoQ to NT$3.1 trillion. June/ July delivered strong YoY (+52%/ 54% YoY), 
and we expect Aug revenues to remain strong at 73% YoY / 11% MoM to NT$1,051bn, 
supported by AI rack server ramp-up, and strong seasonality of the consumer 
electronics business. Maintain Buy (on CL).

Price Target Risks and Methodology – Hon Hai 
Valuation methodology: We are Buy rated on Hon Hai. Our 12-month target price of 
NT$400 is based on a 18.9x 2027E P/E multiple, which is set in line with peers’ PEG&M 
ratio (i.e., P/E vs. forward-year earnings growth and OPM). 
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected ramp-up of the AI server business; (2) 
weaker-than-expected EV total solution performance across EV assembly, design, 
software, and semis; (3) slower-than-expected ramp-up of capacity globally; and (4) 
fiercer-than-expected competition in the consumer electronics EMS business.

Quanta (2382.TW, Neutral): AI server in strong ramp up; PC to decline in 2H26 on high memory cost

Quanta’s Jul revenue was down 5% QoQ / up 131% YoY, or 21% higher than our 
estimate, which we attribute to the strong rack-level AI server shipments, along with the 
stronger than expected PC shipments at 4.5m units. Looking ahead, we expect 
sequential revenue decline in Aug-Sep, mainly on the memory price hike weighing on PC 
end demand. We model sequential revenue increases in Oct-Dec, supported by the next 
generation rack-level AI server shipments ramp up, which carries a higher ASP . Overall, 
we model Quanta’s 3Q / 4Q26E revenues to grow at 85% / 47% YoY, riding on the 
growing AI infrastructure trend. Maintain Neutral.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Quanta 
We are Neutral-rated on Quanta. Our 12-month target price of NT$299 is based on an 
11x 2027E P/E, which references PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple 
correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s performance.  
Key upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery; 2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers.

AVC (3017.TW, Buy): Liquid cooling component ASP and GM upside on the rising complexity of server 
racks; July in line

AVC’s Jul revenue was up 57% YoY / 6% QoQ to NT$19bn, largely in line with our 
estimate. We attribute the strong YoY growth to the strong shipments of AI server liquid 
cooling components, riding on the increasing AI server end demand and a growing liquid 
cooling penetration rate. We model sequential revenue growth in Aug-Dec, considering 
(1) rising shipments of next-generation rack-level AI servers, (2) an increase in liquid 
cooling dollar content in next-generation AI server racks given their growing complexity, 
and (3) a rising liquid cooling penetration rate in both ASIC and GPU AI servers. Maintain 
Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - AVC 
We are Buy-rated on AVC. Our 12-month target price for AVC is based on a near-term 
P/E, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our target price of 
NT$3,986 on a target P/E multiple of 28.2x applied to our forward-year EPS (2027E). 
Our target P/E is derived from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers 
in technology hardware.  
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected adoption of liquid cooling; (2) 
lower-than-expected demand for AI servers; (3) fiercer-than-expected competition; 
and (4) a slower-than-expected recovery in general servers.

Wiwynn (6669.TW, Buy): July revenues sequentially increase; Capex strong increase shows strong AI 
demand

Wiwynn July revenues were in line with our estimates at NT$118bn, driven by solid

### Page 8

Wistron (3231.TW, Buy): July and 2Q26 beat; AI server rack in a strong ramp-up with customer 
expansion and specification upgrades

Wistron’s July revenue was up 61% YoY to NT$308bn, or 30% higher than our estimate, 
reflecting strong AI server rack demand from neo-cloud and enterprises, offsetting the 
impact of the model transition. We expect sequential revenue growth ahead through 
Dec 2026, driven by new GPU AI server racks entering mass production in 4Q26, which 
will drive up the ASP , along with strong AI spending and the expansion of company’s 
customer base. The new GPU AI server racks have various racks across GPU, CPU, and 
LPU, supporting ODM growth in coming quarters. On PC, the company’s July shipments 
were at 1.6m units (-20% MoM / YoY), reflecting modest demand under high memory 
costs. Despite PC shipments likely being negative HoH considering the strong pull-in 
since late 2025, we expect the strong AI server rack ramp-up to offset the impact, 
driving 2H26 revenue up +28% HoH. Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Wistron 
Our 12-month target price of NT$281 is based on a 13.1x 2027E P/E, which is derived 
from PC/server peer EPS growth vs. P/E multiple correlation. We see EPS growth as a 
major factor for the stock’s share price performance.   
Key downside risks: 1) weaker-than-expected PC market recovery, 2) 
slower-than-expected AI server demand ramp-up, and 3) weaker-than-expected 
demand on general servers.

2027E P/E to 2028E NI YoY and OPM. We see EPS growth and OPM as major factors for 
the stock’s share performance.  
Key upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger / weaker-than-expected PC market 
growth; 2) faster / slower-than-expected AI and gaming PC growth; and 3) faster /

Compal (2324.TW, Neutral): AI server capacity in expansion; July better than expected

Compal’s Jul revenue was up 38% YoY / down 15% MoM to NT$81bn, or 10% higher 
than our estimate, reflecting AI servers shipment ramp up. Jul PC shipments was down 
22% YoY / 25% MoM to 1.8m units, reflecting the impact of 1H26 demand pull-in amid 
the rising memory cost. We model sequential revenue growth in Aug-Dec, considering 
the company’s rising AI server shipments, AI server capacity expansion, and PC product 
mix upgrade towards high end model (e.g. AI PC). Overall, we expect Compal’s 3Q / 
4Q26E revenues to grow at 6% / 10% QoQ, benefiting from the rising AI server end 
demand from Neocloud clients. We remain positive on Compal’s business diversification, 
supported by global capacity expansion in the US, Taiwan, and Vietnam, while consumer 
electronics still accounts for the majority of the company’s total revenue, which demand 
could be capped by rising raw material costs (e.g., memory, CPU). Maintain Neutral on 
fair valuation.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Compal 
We are Neutral-rated on Compal with a 12-month target price of NT$41.3. Our target 
price is based on 13.1x 2027E P/E, which is derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth 
vs. P/E multiple correlation. We see EPS growth and market re-rating for ODM 
companies as major factors for the stock’s performance.  
Key upside/downside risks: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market recovery; 
2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger/weaker-than-expected demand for tablets.

Pegatron (4938.TW, Sell): AI server driving 2H26 growth; PC growth capped by rising memory cost; Sell

Pegatron’s Jul revenue was +12% YoY / -6% MoM to NT$86bn, and we attribute the 
MoM decline to the sluggish PC end demand post the demand pull-in in 1H26, causing 
Pegatron’s PC shipments to decline to 0.40m units in Jul from 0.65m units in Jun. We 
model double digit MoM revenue growth in Aug-Sep, considering the low base in Jul, 
followed by modest sequential growth in Oct to Dec, mainly driven by the strong AI 
server shipments growth. Management remains positive on the momentum of AI server 
business in 2H26E, supported by the strong end demand from Neocloud and CSP clients. 
Overall, we expect Pegatron’s 3Q / 4Q26E revenues to grow at +20% / +27% YoY. We are 
positive on Pegatron’s AI server business expansion, while we expect the tight memory 
supply to cap the consumer electronics growth. Maintain Sell.

Earnings revision: Pegatron’s 2Q26 revenue, GM, and Opex ratio are largely in line with 
our estimate, while 2Q26 non-op income is higher than expected, leading 2Q26 net 
income 22% higher than our estimate. We factor in Pegatron’s Jul revenue and 2Q26 
result and raise our 2026-28E net incomes by 10% / 8% / 7%, mainly on higher revenues 
and lower opex ratios. We raise our 2026-28E revenues by 5% / 5% / 4%, mainly on 
higher contribution from AI server business. Our 2026-28E opex ratios are slightly 
revised up, mainly on higher R&D expense to support the AI server specification 
upgrades and PC product mix upgrade towards AI PC.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

### Page 3

WinWay Technology (6515.TW)
07 September 2026 Citi Research
3
WinWay Technology
Valuation
We value WinWay using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum and likely expansion of its addressable
market. Our target price of NT$11,500 is derived from applying c.41x to 2H27E-1H28E EPS, higher than the upper end of its
long-term PE range, which we believe is justified given the company's entrenched position in the high-end AI GPU testing
supply chain, rising contribution from coaxial socket and SLT solutions, and the increasing value of test content per module.

With these structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to value it as an AI/HPC play (rather than a
consumer electronics stock), we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant rating system rates the WinWay stock High Risk based on its historical volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price and our earnings forecasts include: 1) client and

sector concentration – specifically any delays in US AI GPU product roadmap could impact the business outlook for WinWay; 2)
intensifying competition in coaxial and hypersockets from global peers; 3) faster-than-expected testing algorithm
optimization, which could shorten testing durations and reduce the anticipated hardware demand multiplier; and 4) delay of
MEMS Phase 2 mass production.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

richard.hsia@fubon.com 
矽力*-KY (6415 TT)

上半年資料中心營收占比逾 15%

維持買進，目標價 558 元，潛在上漲空間 22% 
1. 生產成本上揚，2Q26 毛利率降至 47.0%，業外支撐 EPS 達 4.02 元。 
2. 主機板、eSSD 與光模組放量，上半年資料中心營收占比逾 15%。 
3. Gen4 預告訂單指引顯示，2027 年汽車、工業(含資料中心)成長擴大。

2Q26 毛利率降至 47.0%，業外支撐 EPS 達 4.02 元 
矽力*-KY 2Q26 合併營收 60.03 億元，QoQ+23.5%，YoY+31.5%，數字小幅優於
富邦及市場預期。產品組合方面，本季分別為消費(36%)、工業(35%)、資訊(9%)、
網通(6%)、 汽車(14%)，其中季成長以工業及汽車逾 50%最佳。 本季毛利率 47.0%，
較前季下滑 1.2 個百分點 ，主因生產成本上揚之影響， 數字低於預期。 業外淨利9.59
億元，絕大部分來自公允價值衡量之金融資產利益 9.59 億元。簡言之，矽力*-KY 
2Q26 營業利益 7.44 億元，稅前淨利 17.02 億元，歸屬母公司稅後淨利 15.71 億
元，EPS 4.02 元，數字優於富邦及市場預期。(以流通在外股本 9.77 億元，每股面
額 2.5 元、流通在外股數 3.908 億股計算，EPS 數字可能和 Cmoney 不同)

主機板、eSSD 與光模組放量，資料中心營收占比逾 15% 
管理層 8 月 28 日法說指出，Gen4 產品受惠 8 吋晶圓產能不足，客戶預告訂單強
勁，工業、汽車未來數季都會有不錯的成長。首先，工業項下的資料中心 2025 年
占比僅約中個位數，1H26 已上揚至 15%，預計 2027 年將達 20%以上。主要挹注
的產品線為 AI 伺服器主機板 、Enterprise SSD 電源供應與光模組等 。其次 ，vcore、
eFuse、信號鏈產品將自下半年開始在客戶端導入市場；電源數位控制器、電源晶
片、driver IC 等是未來兩年資料中心的成長趨動力。汽車方面，2026 年營收比重
預估可達 15-20%；2027 年在歐系客戶及新產品步入量產， 預期比重還會再成長。

下半年營收料逐季高升，全年 EPS 估達 12.12 元 
矽力*-KY 7 月營收 23.57 億元，MoM+13.1%，YoY+58.5%，主要受惠工業、汽
車急單挹注。管理層指引本季預期溫和成長，第四季為全年高峰。其次，生產成本
上揚將持續壓抑短期毛利率表現。因此，富邦預估矽力*-KY 3Q26 合併營收 67.48
億元 ，QoQ+12.4%，YoY+41.7%； 毛利率料大致持平在47.2%， 營業利益估10.46
億元，稅前淨利估 11.79 億元，EPS 估 2.74 元。至於 4Q26 預期營收、毛利率走
揚，EPS 估達 3.40 元，累積全年 EPS 估 12.12 元。

富邦不同之處 
我們對矽力*-KY FY2026-27 年 EPS 預估值分別為 12.12、17.65 元 ， 相較法說前同
業平均值分別約+20%、+1%。

評價與風險 
矽力*-KY 2Q26 毛利率低於預期 ，業外支撐 EPS 優於預期 。 管理層指出後市在 Gen4
產品逐步滲透，營收、毛利率可望漸次走高。公司目前股價約居 FY26 的 38XPER，
居歷史區間(24-72X)中下緣，評價不高，因此我們維持買進的投資評等，目標價設
定 558 元，約為 FY26 的 46.0XPER 或 FY27 的 31.6XPER。矽力*-KY 的投資風險
來自(1)資料中心對 PMIC 需求帶來的外溢效果不如預期；(2)新製程平台營收貢獻
低於管理層之預期等。

### Page 3

台灣股市 | 半導體  
公司速報

請參閱末頁之免責聲明 3

類比 IC 景氣加快回升，矽力-KY 具備產能優勢 
類比 IC 產業需求與價格回溫訊號明顯，主要趨動力來自 AI 基建與應用對電源產品
帶來產能排擠，推升晶圓前後段加工成本上揚。富邦認為此趨勢有利矽力*-KY 基本
面持續擴張 。 首先 ，8 吋晶圓產能緊缺有助矽力*-KY 的 12 吋 Gen4 放量速度加快 ，
目前客戶的預告訂單亦支持此看法。其次，儘管晶圓代工產能吃緊，矽力*-KY 除已
在去年提前下單生產晶圓，並已在 Gen4/Gen5 產品線備妥 2 家代工廠供客戶做選
擇；另外自建的封測產能亦有助緩解部分後段製程交期拉長的問題。簡言之，我們
看好類比 IC 產業正步入上行周期，矽力*-KY 基本面將持續受惠產業趨勢。

FY27：Gen4 佈局效益顯現、PMIC 定價環境改善 
展望 2027 年，HVDC 800V 導入資料中心最終將引起 PMIC 供給吃緊，有利改善
定價環境，推升毛利率重回正常區間。管理層 8 月 28 日法說指出 Gen4 的預告訂
單指引強勁，汽車新客戶明年開始放量 、 資料中心新產品亦持續擴大市占 ， 因此2027
年營收 YoY 成長目標仍為 2-3 成。簡言之，我們預估矽力*-KY 2027 年合併營收達
315.83 億元，YoY+26.7%，毛利率約 49.6%，營業利益估 71.83 億元，稅後淨利
估 68.99 億元，全年 EPS 估 17.65 元。

買進 預估未來6個月內的絕對報酬超過15%

未評等 由於富邦目前與該公司有特定交易或沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等  
評估中 目前正在研議個股的投資評等，將於3到6個月內提供投資評等

產業評等 定義 
優於大盤 預估該產業在未來6個月內會比大盤指數表現突出 
持平 預估該產業在未來6個月內與大盤指數表現相較持平 
劣於大盤 預估該產業在未來6個月內會比大盤指數表現較差

Fubon's Rating System 
Rating Definition 
Buy Expected absolute return to be over 15% within the next 6 months  
Neutral  Expected absolute return to be level within the next 6 months  
Sell  Expected absolute return to be over negative 15% within the next 6 months 
Not Rated (NR)  Pursuant to Fubon acting in deals involving the company or there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an investment rating  
Under Review Fubon is in the process of determining an investment rating and will assign a rating within the 3- to 6-month horizon

Sector Rating Definition 
Overweight Sector expected to outperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months 
Market Weight  Sector expected to perform in line with the broader market averages within the next 6-12 months 
Underweight  Sector expected to underperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months.

### Page 1

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
6609 瀧澤科(TAKISAWA)

報告日期： 2026/09/02 研 究 員：洪  國 峰 (Titan Horng)  
壹、投 資 建 議

一. 公告 2Q26 稅後 EPS 0.28 元 
瀧澤科 2Q26 營收金額 6.63 億元， YoY+1.07% ，QoQ+34.88% ，
營業利益 0.30 億元， YoY+2.89% ，較 1Q26 由虧轉盈，稅後盈餘
0.20 億元，較 2Q25 與 1Q26 由虧轉盈 ，稅後EPS 0.28 元。1H26
稅後 EPS 0.12 元，優於去年同期 -0.74 元。

二. 預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 0.56 元 
瀧澤科 07/2026 營收金額 2.39 億元，YoY+62.79% ，MoM-2.3% 。 
在手訂單己有小幅回升，宏遠預估瀧澤科 3Q26 營收金額 6.99 億
元，YoY+81.06% ，QoQ+5.4% ， 預估營業利益0.33 億 元，較3Q25
由虧轉盈 ，QoQ+8.2% ， 預估稅後盈餘0.40 億元 ，YoY+867.26% ，
QoQ+97.36% ，預估稅後 EPS 0.56 元。

三. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.10 元 
瀧澤科 2026 年在日本瀧澤在母公司日本 Nidec 集團資源挹注 ， 帶
動 CNC 車床與 OEM 車床同步成長，推升營運走高。宏遠預估瀧
澤科 2026 年營收金額 25.00 億元， YoY+22.64% ，預估營業利益
0.70 億元，由虧轉盈，預估稅後盈餘 0.80 億元，由虧轉盈，預估
稅後 EPS 1.10 元。

四. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 1.80 元 
瀧澤科 2027 年在 Nidec 集團效益持續始發酵 ， 加上透過集團銷售
平台與代理商，逐步將產品推到集團的供應商與客戶，其中瀧澤
科己接到集團內的水冷散熱加工設備訂單，有助整體出貨量持續
成長，推升 2027 年營運走高。宏遠預估瀧澤科 2027 年營收金額
27.50 億元， YoY+10.00% ，預估營業利益 1.44 億元，
YoY+105.18% ，預估稅後盈餘 1.30 億元， YoY+63.46% ，預估稅
後 EPS 1.80 元。

五. 投資建議買進，目標價 50 元 
觀察瀧澤科歷史 PER 6~17X ，台灣瀧澤科在日本 Nidec 集團資源
整合效率發酵 ， 己成功切入水冷散熱設備供應鏈 ， 有助評價提升，
故投資建議買進，目標價 50 元（ 28X 2027 年稅後 EPS ）。

貳、公 司 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
瀧澤科成立於 1971 年，資本額 7.25 億元，瀧澤科為日本瀧澤科
持股 51% 子公司（日本瀧澤科己於 2023 年被 NIDEC 收購下市）。
主要是以生產 CNC 車床為主， 1998 年開始生產 PCB 鑽孔機，瀧
澤科主要以 CNC 車床 、PCB 鑽孔機與替日本瀧澤科代工生產 CNC
車床為營收三大來源。 CNC 車床主要應用於汽機車模具零組件加
工、國防航太與其他各種機器類零件加工為主，其中以汽車模具
業占比最大，約占 40~50% 。營收地區方面，亞洲地區 60~61% 、
歐洲比重 16~17% 、美洲地區比重 12~13% 、台灣內銷 9~10% 。

二. 2026 年營運概況及展望

預估 2026 年 CNC 車床營收金額 19.00 億元， YoY+15.1%

瀧澤科 CNC 車床主要用於機車、汽車、自行車零件加工、國防航
太工業的零件加工，屬切削工具機。瀧澤科 2025 年 CNC 車床出
貨量 998 台， YoY-11.99% ，貢獻 2025 年營收金額 16.51 億元，
YoY-17.1%。 營收衰退主因在於受到對等關稅 ， 導致全球經濟未見
明顯復甦回溫，企業資本支出縮減所致。

母公司日本瀧澤己被日本 Nidec 併購 ，Nidec 為專業馬達製造廠，
生產電動車及電子馬達，基於對工具機有需求，相關客戶也需要
用到工具機，過去三年公開收購日本 OKK 及三菱、義大利 PAMA
三家工具機廠，預期在集團資源整合，將有助台灣瀧澤 CNC 訂單
增加，預估瀧澤科 CNC 車床 2026 年出貨量 1100 台，
YoY+10.22% ，貢獻 2026 年營收金額 19.00 億元， YoY+15.1% 。

預期 2027 年，在 Nidec 集團資源持續整合 ， 將有助於瀧澤科CNC
車床出貨持續增加，另外在中國大陸機器人產業發展熱絡帶動
下，瀧澤科己接獲中國大陸減速機與馬達公司訂單，預計 2027 年
開始正式出貨，預估瀧澤科 CNC 車床 2027 年出貨量 1180 台，
YoY+7.27% ，貢獻 2027 年營收金額 20.9 億元， YoY+100.9% 。

預估 2026 年 OEM 車床營收金額 4.50 億元， YoY+124.9%

OEM 車床方面，瀧澤科為日本母公司株式瀧澤 OEM 代工。瀧澤
科 2025 年 OEM 車床出貨量 444 台，YoY-54.74% ，貢獻 2025 年
營收金額 2.45 億元， YoY-54.5% ，營收衰退主因在於日本瀧澤
2025 年受到美國關稅影響 ， 訂單大幅減少 ， 間接影響OEM 訂單。

宏观经济点评

8 月外贸数据点评：出口延续高增，结构分化边际
收敛

 魏争 分析师  沈夏宜 分析师

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
增速由 7 月的 6.9%升至 12.3%，对出口拉动由 0.64 个百分点提高至 1.06
个百分点，传统产品与优势制造业之间的分化有所收窄。 
量价上看，出口价格支撑广度扩散 。8 月，出口的价格支撑进一步增强，
并由电子、装备及能源产品向部分传统消费品扩散 。一方面，电子、装备
及能源产品出口高增仍主要由价格贡献。AI 产业景气及地缘局势扰动背景
下，集成电路、手机、船舶和成品油出口金额分别增长 129.8%、
29.6%、21.0%和 80.5%。其中，出口价格同比分别上涨 149.7%、
50.0%、60.8%和 60.1%，出口数量同比增速分别为-7.9%、-13.6%、-
24.7%、 12.8%，指向出口金额高增主主因价格上涨支撑。另一方面，价
格支撑开始向部分传统消费品扩散。8 月，箱包和鞋靴出口金额分别增长
11.1%和 11.4%，较 7 月的 7.4%和 2.7%均有所加快，其中，出口价格分
别上涨 12.1%和 18.1%，而出口数量分别下降 0.9%和 5.7%，指向价格上
涨成为部分消费品出口修复的主要驱动，一定程度掩盖了出口数量回落。 
进口增速回升，价格支撑仍强，主要集中在集成电路、铜矿石和能源品 。
8 月，进口同比增长 28.2%，较 7 月提高 0.7 个百分点。结构上看，8 月
进口呈现四点特征。一是，机电产品仍为增长的主要动力，集成电路价格
贡献显著。机电产品进口增长 51.7%，拉动整体进口 20.4 个百分点，其
中，集成电路和自动数据处理设备分别增长 83.6%和 209.1%，合计拉动
19.7 个百分点；集成电路进口数量仅增长 6.7%，价格上涨 72.2%，金额
高增主要由价格推动。二是，农产品进口温和增长，量价均有贡献。农产
品进口增长 5.4%，拉动整体进口 0.5 个百分点。其中，水果、粮食分别
增长 20.2%和 13.1%，量价均有支撑；大豆增长 7.3%，主要受价格上涨
8.5%带动。三是金属资源品进口改善，铜矿价格贡献较为突出。铁矿石、
铜矿石和稀土合计拉动进口 1.1 个百分点。其中，铜矿石进口增长
24.7%，数量下降 9.1%，价格上涨 37.2%；稀土进口增长 25.4%，主要
由数量增长 36.7%带动；四是能源进口呈现价增量减。原油、成品油和天
然气进口价格分别上涨 18.9%、28.8%和 22.6%，但数量分别下降
23.4%、32.8%和 12.8%，三者合计拖累整体进口约 1.0 个百分点。 
往后看，出口韧性短期仍有支撑，但高增的持续性 取决于订单、数量和价
格。一方面，全球制造业仍处于扩张区间，中国新出口订单指数明显回
升，AI 资本开支及优势制造业海外需求尚未出现明显降温，传统消费品出
口修复也意味着出口支撑较 7 月有所扩散。另一方面，8 月部分商品出口
高增较多依赖出口价格上升，对美出口加快也受到低基数支撑，名义出口
增速对真实外需强度可能存在一定放大。后续一是关注全球制造业新出口
订单和 AI 产业链需求能否延续，二是关注主要出口产品价格能否维持，
三是关注传统消费品修复能否从少数品类进一步扩散。

风险提示：AI 产业链景气回落；全球制造业修复不及预期；地缘冲突升级
风险；中美贸易摩擦反复风险；美元流动性收紧风险；海外经济超预期放
缓风险。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

Document 180: AI瓶颈大辩论：2027 顶峰瓶颈 vs 2028 产能大释放与定价崩塌

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI瓶颈大辩论：2027 顶峰瓶颈 vs 2028 产能大释放与定价崩塌.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

分析师 Ben Bajarin 认为 AI 算⼒需求持续增⻓， 2027 年将是全⾏业供给约束最极致的 " 瓶颈
点 " ， 2028 年供需将维持略紧平衡； Jay Goldberg 则警告历史周期铁律，台积电等天量产
能或引发定价权崩塌与⽑利率均值回归。两⼈共识： AI 供应链 " 闭眼买⼊即暴涨 " 时代已终
结，投资者须精选具备跨周期定价韧性的核⼼资产。
摘要：
AI 算⼒军备竞赛正驶向⼀个关键拐点。
随着博通、英伟达等科技巨头在 2025 年底⾄ 2026 年初与供应链签署⼤规模⻓期锁单协议，半导
体⾏业正在经历史上规模最⼤的⼀轮资本扩张，⽽这笔投资的真正落地，将在 2028 年引爆⼀场
关于供需格局的根本性争论。
9 ⽉ 9 ⽇，深度⾏业播客节⽬《 The Circuit 》上科技分析师 Ben Bajarin 与 Jay Goldberg 就AI 芯⽚
周期展开正⾯交锋。
Ben Bajarin 认为，2027 年将是全⾏业供给约束最极致的 " 瓶颈点 " ， 2028 年新产能虽⼤规模释

放，但持续扩张的 AI 需求将使供需维持在略微偏紧状态，不会出现断崖式过剩。
Jay Goldberg 则援引半导体⾏业 50 年周期规律提出反向警告：台积电、美光、三星与 SK 海⼒⼠
正在同步推进的天量产能，⼀旦 2028 年集中出笼，将触发定价权崩塌与⽑利率均值回归的惨烈
出清。
两⼈尽管在 2028 年的落点上存在根本分歧，但在⼀点上达成明确共识：过去两年 " 闭眼买 AI 供应
链即暴涨 " 的时代已经终结。随着瓶颈从单⼀环节向多极分化演进，投资者必须以更严苛的标准
甄别真正具备跨周期定价韧性的核⼼资产。
系统性约束：短板效应主导芯⽚出货上限
理解这场争论的前提，在于半导体供应链的⼀个基本逻辑：整个系统的实际出货量，由最薄弱
的单⼀瓶颈环节决定。
即便某⼀环节的 GPU 或内存供应充⾜，基板、 MLCC 、晶圆测试等任⼀环节告急，总产出便⽆
法提升。
这⼀ " 短板效应 " 直接解释了当前供应链的紧张格局。基板、电源与模拟半导体、晶圆代⼯以及存
储供应商，历史上普遍采取保守投资策略，在未获得客⼾⻓期需求明确背书之前，不会贸然启

动绿地新建产能。
直到 2025 年底⾄ 2026 年初，博通、英伟达及各⼤云服务商向供应链提交了规模可观、条款严苛
的⻓期锁单协议后，主要供应商才全⼒启动新⼀轮资本开⽀。
由于⼀座晶圆⼚、先进封装产线（如台积电 CoWoS ）或⾼端 ABF 基板⼚的建设与⽆尘室认证天
然需要 24 ⾄ 36 个⽉，这批投资的实际批量产出，最早要到 2028 年才能形成有效供给。
这条时间线，是双⽅争论的共同起点。
Ben Bajarin ： 2027 年供给绝顶， 2028 年温和回归
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

Ben Bajarin 的核⼼论据建⽴在物理建设周期的不可压缩性之上。
他认为， 2026 ⾄ 2027 年间，旧产能已被锁满、新产线尚未投产，全球先进制程与先进封装将处
于史上最极端的供给瓶颈期。
各⼤云⼚商届时将别⽆选择，只能接受任何能买到的算⼒配额 ——2027 年可能成为半导体历史
上供给约束最严峻的⼀年。
对于 2028 年， Ben Bajarin 并不认同 " 产能洪⽔ " 的悲观叙事。
他指出，随着 AI 模型从纯⽂本转向⾼清视频、实时⾳频、 3D ⽣成与⻓程推理， Token 词元消耗呈
指数级增⻓，需求端的扩张速度⾜以快速消化新释放的产能。
在他的预判中， 2028 年整个市场充其量是⾛出 " 绝对短缺 " ，回到供需略微偏紧（需求 / 供给约为
105% ⾄ 110% ）的良性状态，不会发⽣断崖式过剩。
他还提出当前的供给瓶颈在某种意义上发挥了 " 安全阀 " 的作⽤，延缓了过度投资的发⽣。
他将这⼀ AI 基础设施周期与历史上的铁路、运河、 3G 及移动互联⽹建设热潮相⽐较，指出此前

这些领域均出现了严重的过度建设，⽽ AI 算⼒这⼀轮的关键区别在于，即便需求明确存在，⽣产
设施与数据中⼼的建设速度同样受到物理限制，供给约束本⾝就在制约过度投资。
他还补充，在企业级 AI 部署中，⾼昂的 Token 成本与有限的算⼒获取渠道，也在客观上放缓了从
实验环境向⽣产环境迁移的进程。
Jay Goldberg ：历史周期铁律， 2028 年定价权或遭反噬
Jay Goldberg 的反向预警来⾃对半导体⾏业五⼗年资本周期的历史归纳。他的核⼼判断是：每
⼀个上⾏周期中，所有参与者都会⾼喊 " 这次不⼀样 " ，但每⼀次都以产能严重过剩收场。
当前在建项⽬的规模令⼈警惕：
Jay Goldberg 还特别点出 " 重复预订 " （ Double/Triple Booking ）的历史规律。
在芯⽚短缺期，苹果、⾼通、云⼚商等客⼾为确保拿到货，往往会同时向多个供应商提交重叠
甚⾄翻倍的产能意向。⼀旦供给开始释放、交期缩短，这部分虚胖的 " 幽灵订单 " 会瞬间被取消。
在定价层⾯， Jay Goldberg 认为，英伟达当前 70% 以上的超⾼⽑利率、博通的⾼昂 ASIC 溢价，

本质上是供给短缺制造的稀缺性溢价。
台积电在全球推进近 20 座新晶圆⼚与封装基地；
美光全球在建 7 座⼤型存储⼚；
三星与SK 海⼒⼠在 HBM 领域的投资规模同样激进；
此外还有受政府补贴催熟的英特尔等⼚商产能。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
1 条评论
到 2028 年，当⼤量晶圆⼚机器全部开动、固定资产折旧形成巨⼤成本压⼒时，代⼯⼚和芯⽚设
计商为维持设备稼动率（ Utilization Rate ），势必展开激烈价格战，届时硬件⽑利率将⾯临残酷
的均值回归。
投资共识：精选时代取代普涨逻辑
尽管两⼈在 2028 年的终局判断上分歧显著，但在⼀个关键的市场判断上，双⽅⽴场⼀致： AI 供
应链 " 闭眼买⼊即获超额回报 " 的窗⼝已经关闭。
过去两年，任何业务与先进封装、 HBM 、 GPU 或⾼速光模块沾边的公司，股价均⼤幅受益于单
⼀瓶颈带来的板块性溢价。
但从当前到 2027 ⾄ 2028 年，供应链瓶颈正在从单⼀环节（如早期的 CoWoS 封装）向多个维度分
散演进。
随着联发科、 Marvell 等定制计算玩家加速布局，以及 2028 年⼤规模产能次第释放，缺乏不可替

代软件⽣态或核⼼ IP 护城河的硬件供应商，将在 2027 年底⾄ 2028 年⾯临⽑利压缩与去库存的双
重压⼒。
两位分析师的共同结论是：投资者不能再将半导体供应链视为同质化整体，必须聚焦真正具备
跨周期定价韧性的核⼼环节，⽅能在这场史上最⼤规模产能扩张落地后的分化格局中占据有利
位置。

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Commodities: From Inflation Hedge to Structural Growth Asset
Anna Snider, Managing Director and Head of Investment Manager Selection
(Due Diligence)
A broader role in portfolios: For decades, commodities occupied a relatively narrow
role in portfolios: protection against inflation when rising prices eroded the value
of traditional assets. That function remains important, but the investment case is

broadening. Commodities now sit at the intersection of geopolitical fragmentation,
supply-chain security, reshoring, inventory rebuilding, and the buildout of AI and digital
infrastructure. Together, these forces are turning parts of the asset class from purely
cyclical exposures into potential long-term growth beneficiaries.
The diversification case is also becoming more relevant. In Q1, when inflation concerns
peaked, the Bloomberg Commodity Index returned close to 25%, outperforming both

Equities and Fixed Income. At the same time, the relationship among major asset
classes has shifted. Equity and Fixed Income correlations have tightened in recent years,
while commodities have moved in the opposite direction: over the trailing five years,
their correlation with both Equities and bonds has been negative. That combination of
inflation sensitivity and differentiated return behavior can make commodities useful
beyond a simple tactical hedge.

AI infrastructure is a physical buildout: AI is often viewed through semiconductors,
software platforms, and hyperscale data centers. Yet the infrastructure required to
deploy AI is extraordinarily commodity intensive. It requires continuous electricity,
generation capacity, transmission lines, substations, transformers, cooling systems,
networking hardware and structural materials. In that sense, AI is not only a computing
revolution. It is also an energy, infrastructure, and critical-materials investment cycle.

Power is the first constraint. Data centers require reliable, around-the-clock electricity.
Natural gas remains a practical source of incremental generation in the U.S., while
nuclear power is emerging as a strategic long-term option for hyperscalers seeking
stable baseload supply. Solar and wind can add capacity and support decarbonization
goals, but grid reliability and storage become more important as power demand rises.
That supports investment in batteries, backup power and grid-scale storage, with lithium

and graphite among the relevant inputs.
The buildout also increases demand for industrial metals. Copper is a clear beneficiary
because it is essential to transmission, transformers, cooling systems and data-center
wiring. Aluminum and iron ore are used in facilities, electrical equipment, transmission
networks and supporting infrastructure. Semiconductor fabrication adds another layer
of demand through industrial gases such as helium and neon, high-purity quartz for

wafers and optics, and gallium and germanium for advanced chips, photonics, and
telecommunications. AI-enabled automation and robotics also depend on permanent
magnets that use rare earth elements, including neodymium-praseodymium and
dysprosium-terbium.
Demand has moved faster than supply: This demand surge resembles earlier cycles
in semiconductors and memory, where expectations pulled spending forward. Metals
and mining companies are already seeing earnings growth and margin expansion,

yet the industry has struggled to keep pace with demand. The imbalance matters
because commodity supply is slow to respond. Production of many strategic materials
is geographically concentrated, permitting timelines are lengthy, recycling capacity is
limited, and substitution can remain years from commercial viability. New mines and
processing facilities can take a decade or more to reach production.
Investment Implications
Commodities may increasingly serve

three portfolio purposes: inflation
protection, diversification from
traditional assets, and participation in
long-term infrastructure and resource-
demand growth, supporting a diversified
allocation across direct commodities
and select producer Equities rather than
a single directional trade.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Governments are also taking a larger role through industrial policy, export controls,
stockpiling and efforts to secure critical supply chains. Restrictions involving gallium,
germanium, graphite and other strategic materials underscore how geopolitics can affect
availability and pricing. As a result, the current cycle may produce greater scarcity and
pricing power than demand growth alone would suggest.

So far, this dynamic has been most visible in industrial metals, but the same forces could
extend into agriculture. Reshoring changes where food and inputs are produced and
processed, while inventory rebuilding can lift demand for fertilizer, fuel, machinery and
storage. Agriculture has not yet experienced the same earnings and pricing impact as
metals, but supply-chain resilience could make it a broader beneficiary over time.
Cyclical forces still matter: The structural thesis does not eliminate near-term

volatility. Energy remains a key transmission mechanism for inflation because oil and
natural gas underpin transportation, manufacturing and industrial production. Geopolitical
disruptions can cascade through supply chains, raising costs and moving both commodity
futures and spot prices. Currency dynamics matter as well. A stronger U.S. dollar can
restrain commodity prices by increasing costs for non-U.S. buyers, while renewed dollar
weakness could support emerging-market demand and reprice dollar-denominated

assets.
A stabilization of geopolitical tensions could create a more constructive cyclical backdrop
through lower energy volatility, improved business confidence and stronger capital
investment. Conversely, renewed conflict can produce sharp and uneven moves across
the complex. These crosscurrents reinforce the value of treating commodities as a
diversified allocation rather than a single directional trade.
Gold, implementation and the portfolio case: Gold is fundamentally different from

most industrial commodities, but it deserves attention. Its traditional relationships with
interest rates and the dollar have been less reliable lately. Even so, sustained central bank
and exchange-traded fund buying points to durable long-term demand despite bouts of
volatility such as the selloff earlier this year. Gold also tended to move inversely with oil
during the height of the Iran conflict, illustrating how different commodities can respond

differently to the same geopolitical shock.
Investors can access the theme through commodity futures or producer Equities. Direct
commodity exposure is generally the clearer vehicle for inflation protection, scarcity, and
geopolitical supply shocks. Producer equities can offer greater upside when commodity
prices rise faster than operating costs because earnings and margins expand, but that
leverage is asymmetric. Company-specific execution, financing, cost and valuation risks

can still cause Equities to underperform the underlying commodity. A blended approach
may capture both physical scarcity and corporate operating leverage.
Ultimately, commodities remain sensitive to energy markets, currencies, growth, and
geopolitics, but their portfolio role is expanding. They can provide inflation protection and
diversification while offering exposure to AI infrastructure, electrification, energy security,
defense modernization, reshoring and, potentially, agricultural supply-chain investment.

The strongest beneficiaries of the AI trade may therefore extend well beyond technology
companies to the materials and power systems that make deployment possible.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Rising Global Interest Rates and International Markets
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Strong earnings growth has so far delivered a fourth consecutive year of double-digit
gains for global Equity indexes in 2026, propelling them to new all-time highs. But a
growing risk throughout the year has been the increase in government bond yields, which

have also broken out to multi-decade highs across a number of the major markets. The
U.S. Treasury will this week begin its expanded Treasury securities buyback program
announced in mid-August in response to rising rates. But 10-year yields across all
the major G10 countries have moved higher, in many cases by as much as or more
than in the U.S. market. Against a backdrop of above-trend growth in nominal gross
domestic product (GDP), high energy prices and elevated inflation, investors appear to

be reassessing monetary policy expectations, particularly in Europe where most of the
2026 increase in 10-year yields has been driven by inflation breakevens (Exhibit 1). Fixed
Income markets are consequently looking for policy rate hikes from the European Central
Bank later this week and potentially from the Bank of Japan later next month.
The rise in global interest rates has also come alongside an expansion in fiscal policy
across the major markets. In Europe, 2026 and 2027 are expected to be the peak

spending years for defense and infrastructure expenditure plans unveiled last year. The
so-called Readiness 2030 package allocates €800 billion to increased European Union
defense spending as it looks to move toward its 5% of GDP target by 2035. And at the
same time, the German government relaxation of its national debt brake has directed
€500 billion toward funding for investment in clean energy, transportation and digital
infrastructure. Similarly in Japan, the government supermajority is looking to enact a

new stimulus aimed at raising investment levels across 17 strategic sectors such as
semiconductors, defense and shipbuilding, in addition to a planned two-year cut to the
consumption tax on food from April 2027. And in the UK, the new central government
leadership is also likely to announce new spending measures when its first national
budget is released next month. To the extent that increased public investment spending
can lift productivity and support nominal GDP growth in Europe and Japan, the rise in

yields need not undermine economic growth or derail the equity market advance. But
higher rates, especially in response to supply-driven energy price inflation, nonetheless
remain a risk for developed market equity returns going into the end of the year.
Exhibit 1: Global Bond Yields in Major Markets have Risen by as Much or More
than U.S. Yields in 2026.

For many emerging markets the picture looks quite different. In contrast with the U.S.,
Japan and Europe, inflation rates are generally well below central bank targets outside
a limited number of resource exporters in Latin America and the booming AI-linked
economies of Korea and Taiwan (Exhibit 2). As a result, policy rate expectations over

the coming quarters are stable or lower for several emerging markets (including China,
Malaysia, Thailand, Poland, Brazil and Mexico) according to Bloomberg consensus forecasts.
Investment Implications
Ongoing strength in economic growth
and corporate earnings should support
global equity markets even in the face
of rising rates. For Emerging Markets
(EM) Equities in particular, contained
inflation, flat to lower local policy rate
expectations and stronger fundamentals

should limit the impact. And we
continue to favor EM Equities over
International Developed Markets, where
inflation levels are generally still above
target, central banks are expected
to raise rates and investors will be
watching for fiscal sustainability as
governments implement expansionary
budget plans.

Historically, rising U.S. interest rates have been a major headwind for emerging markets.
But looking ahead in the current cycle, we still expect the impact of potential Federal
Reserve (Fed) rate hikes to be lower than in the past. Over recent years, the association
between tighter Fed policy and weaker EM returns appears to have diminished. EM
Equities for example were resilient in the face of aggressive U.S. rate increases in 2022
and 2023, broadly keeping pace with the rest of the world. This is in sharp contrast to

prior cycles in which tighter Fed policy has resulted in capital flight, deleveraging and
falling asset values across the emerging world. Particularly during the crisis periods of
the 1980s and 1990s—but also more recently during the 2015 taper tantrum—U.S.
monetary tightening has tended to attract capital away from riskier emerging countries
(particularly those with large current account deficits), reducing support for local asset
prices and exchange rates.

Associated strength in the U.S. dollar has typically been an additional hurdle. For those
emerging economies with fixed exchange rates, a loss of export competitiveness has
further undermined current account positions, forcing central banks to run down their
foreign exchange reserves. And where exchange rates are floating, foreign currency
debts have risen in local currency terms, resulting in widespread defaults, debt
downgrades, recessions and additional exchange rate depreciation.

But starting in the years that followed the 1997-1998 Asian financial crisis and
continuing into the current cycle, the fundamentals across much of the EM universe have
improved. Fixed exchange rates have been abandoned. EM foreign currency reserves have
roughly doubled as a share of GDP . The share of EM debt denominated in U.S. dollars
has fallen significantly, led by the private sector across households and corporates. And
for most countries, current account deficits have narrowed or disappeared entirely. As a

result, emerging markets have become much more insulated from the direction of U.S.
interest rates on a structural basis, and this has only been reinforced by the AI-driven
revaluation of key Asian markets in 2026. Greater concentration in Korea and Taiwan has
increased the EM weighting in the surplus Asia-Pacific region to 83% as at the end of
August—up from 81% at the start of the year and 71% a decade ago.
Above-trend growth in nominal GDP and ongoing strength in corporate earnings should

support global equity markets even in the face of rising interest rates. But for emerging
Equities in particular, contained inflation relative to central bank targets, flat to lower
local policy rate expectations and stronger economic fundamentals should limit the
impact from higher global rates. We continue to favor EM Equities over International
Developed Markets, where inflation levels are generally still above target, central banks
are expected to raise interest rates and investors will be watching for fiscal sustainability

as governments implement expansionary budget plans.

### Page 6

6 of 8 September 8, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The One-Offs Inflating Q2 Earnings
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Headline Q2 earnings numbers look almost too good to be true. And in a sense, they are.
With nearly all of S&P 500 companies’ results reported, EPS growth is tracking around
50% year-over-year (YoY)—not just an acceleration from Q1 but a second consecutive
quarter above 20%. Chiefly skewing the headline number are massive mark-to-market

gains on private investments for a handful of mega cap technology companies like
Alphabet and Amazon that booked outsized non-operating gains tied to their stakes in
Anthropic, while Microsoft benefited from its OpenAI investment. Strip those out, and
adjusted EPS growth notches down to 31%. The point being, after being sliced and
diced, 30%+ is well above any long-run historical earnings growth rate.
The one-off nature of earnings strength wasn’t limited to investment gains. A second,

less-discussed boost was tariff refunds. As Exhibit 3 illustrates, revenues from tariffs
steadily climbed from early 2025 through mid-2026 until the International Emergency
Economic Powers Act (IEEPA) tariff ruling which initiated the refund process. As
refunds were disbursed, customs receipts turned net negative in June and July, with
refund payments exceeding total tariff collections. Customs and Border Protection cash
withdrawals of more than $100 billion in refunds have now been paid—60% of the $165

billion in total eligible claims.
The recipients: the “importers of record,” or the dozens of S&P 500 companies that
have disclosed billions in refunds over the past two quarters; Apple alone received $2.19
billion, adding $0.11 to per-share earnings to their quarter. Other disclosed refunds
include Nike with $986 million, Amazon with $640 million, and General Motors refunded
$500 million (although accounting treatment and use of proceeds has varied).

The boost of investment gains and/or refunds may not be repeatable in future quarters.
Perhaps more telling is that the median company’s revenue grew roughly 15% YoY, the
strongest gain in years, corroborating that there is operating momentum rather than
just an accounting mirage for Q2 earnings. Margins, too, as a percent of GDP are at
their highest back to 1950, hovering north of 22%, see Exhibit 4. That this is occurring
amid rising rates and at a time of geopolitical uncertainty/disruption, rather than a post-

recessionary recovery, makes the results that much more impressive.
The Path Forward: Questions remain about the sustainability of these elevated growth
rates, although consensus expects EPS growth to stay above 20% in both Q3 and Q4.
If achieved, every quarter this year would see 20%-plus EPS growth. On the back of
brighter outlooks from Corporate America in recent weeks, full-year earnings growth
will decelerate into the mid-teens in 2027, but that moderation comes off a high base

of 2026. For now, the breadth of earnings growth is the encouraging story as 10 of
the 11 sectors reported growth (Energy and Communication Services leading the way,
Healthcare the only detractor). That’s a reflection of strong core businesses growing
revenues into 2027’s tougher YoY comparisons.
Investment Implications
Underlying earnings-per-share (EPS)
growth of around 30%, stripped
of investment gains, is historically
exceptional. It remains that robust

corporate earnings and positive
revisions have helped Equities absorb
concerns around the recent rise
in yields.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.
Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and
investment advisory services, including the type of advice and assistance provided, the fees charged, and the rights and obligations of the parties. It is important to understand the differences,
particularly when determining which service or services to select. For more information about these services and their differences, speak with your Merrill financial advisor.

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.

Document 208: Data centres and renewables plug the widening public infrastructure gap (2026-09-10)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Data centres and renewables plug the widening public infrastructure gap (2026-09-10).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 18
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #construction
Data centres and renewables plug the widening public
infrastructure gap
By Pat Bustamante
Senior Economist, Westpac
14:51 August 26 2026 Share
Construction activity declined 2.1%qtr in Q2 2026. Outside of mining-related volatility,
activity remained solid, driven by residential building (+1.7%qtr), private non-residential
building (+2.3%qtr, includes data centres) and electricity related construction (+7.5%qtr in
nominal terms).

### Page 2

Construction activity declined 2.1%qtr in Q2 2026 to be 2.7% higher in year-ended terms. This was not
far off the 1.0%qtr fall expected by Westpac Economics, but considerably weaker than the +0.4%
growth expected by the market consensus. The weakness in the aggregate number was driven by the
unwind of a mining related spike (related to the installation of a floating production unit for the
Scarborough Energy Project in WA), which in turn was driven by the ABS’ practice in this measure of

recognising installed imported structures on a cash basis.
Outside of this, activity was solid, rising an estimated 1.6%qtr on the back of a 1.7%.qtr pickup in
residential building and a 2.3%qtr lift in private non-residential building activity. Residential building is
up 9.1% in year-ended terms – the strongest outcome in a decade (June quarter 2016). Supporting this
residential activity, we saw the number of dwellings under construction lift 10.7% over the year to Q1,

with the recent run of approvals suggesting smaller gains going forward. Public infrastructure work
declined for a third consecutive quarter, down 2.1%qtr and 3.9%yr, consistent with our view that the
infrastructure pipeline has passed its peak.
What does this mean for the National Accounts?
Today’s release is the first of a set of partials which will feed into next week’s June quarter 2026
National Accounts. While the headline numbers suggest downside risks for the Q2 GDP outcome, this

is not the case - if anything, today’s numbers alone point to stronger growth in residential and new
buildings activity over the quarter.

### Page 3

Today’s construction work data is recorded on a value of work done/installed basis. This means capital
expenditure on machinery, equipment and structures is allocated to the quarter in which the capital
items are ready for use. However, the National Accounts are measured on an accrual basis using
‘progress payments’, meaning the value of the investment is recorded incrementally as the project
progresses not just when the project is complete.

What about inflationary pressures and the impact of the data centre boom?
The spike in construction costs during the pandemic was largely synchronised across dwellings, non-
residential buildings and engineering construction. Today, however, cost pressures differ as demand
profiles across these segments diverge. 
While rebounding in the quarter, possibly on the back of the large pick-up in new electricity generation
and distribution work, engineering construction costs have been moderating as major public
infrastructure programs wind down. In contrast, dwelling construction costs are accelerating as activity
in this segment picks up. Non-residential building costs appear to be going sideways, rather than
significantly reaccelerating, with strength in private investment, including data centres, offset by a
slowdown in public projects.
At 3.9%yr, aggregate construction cost growth is toward the upper end of what was recorded over
2018-19. This raises questions about whether the strong demand in pockets like data centre
investment are contributing to broader inflationary pressures. For now, there is limited evidence of this

### Page 4

happening. However, construction cost deflators continue to firm in year-ended terms, suggesting that
the disinflation anticipated by the RBA may take longer to materialise.
How this plays out going forward depends on how much the roll-off in public construction activity makes
room for the pickup in private non-residential building work, particularly data centres, and private
engineering construction associated with the renewable energy transition. Data on work yet to be done

suggest this handover will keep the pipeline balanced: despite the substantial increase in approved
data centre projects, the value of total construction work yet to be done has remained broadly stable as
a share of nominal GDP.
The detail
Residential construction outperformed, recording a 1.7%qtr increase in Q2, following an upwardly
revised 0.6%qtr rise in Q1 2026 (previously estimated as a 0.6% fall). Growth was driven by a 1.7%qtr
increase in new residential construction and a 2.0%qtr lift in renovation activity.

In year-ended terms, residential construction is up 9.1%yr, the strongest growth rate since the June
quarter of 2016. This suggests the sector is responding to growing demand by expanding output,
despite a prolonged period of elevated interest rates and ongoing cost pressures.
The strength in residential construction suggests supply is finally responding to Australia's housing
shortage. However, with approvals easing and demand expected to moderate, the current pace of

expansion may prove difficult to sustain, particularly given rising construction costs.

### Page 5

Engineering construction (EC) declined 6.0%qtr in Q2 2026, leaving activity 5.1% lower in year-
ended terms. However, this weakness largely reflected the unwind of a mining-related spike. Excluding
this volatility, engineering construction grew a healthy 2.0%qtr in the quarter.

### Page 6

As foreshadowed in this month's Market Outlook, the public infrastructure pipeline appears to have
passed its peak as major transport projects across Australia's east coast, particularly in NSW and
Victoria, are completed. Consistent with this, public engineering work fell a further 2.1%qtr in Q2 and is
now almost 4.0% lower than a year ago.
In contrast, private engineering construction remains robust. After adjusting for mining-related volatility,

private EC work grew almost 6.0%qtr in Q2. The value of private work on electricity generation and
distribution, which is largely driven by renewable energy projects such as wind and solar
developments, increased by around 7.5qtr.
Looking ahead, the private engineering construction pipeline remains supported by structural
investment themes, particularly the energy transition and the nationwide roll-out of data centres.
However, constraints around utility provision, grid connections and supporting infrastructure pose a key

risk to the timely delivery of these projects.

### Page 7

Non-residential building construction increased 0.5%qtr in Q2 2026, leaving activity 11.5% higher
over the year. Strength in the private sector, up 2.3%qtr and 18.4%yr, more than offset a 3.9%qtr
decline in public activity. Private non-residential construction is now running at its strongest level since
early 2008, supported by the need to accommodate a larger population and the ongoing data centre
build-out.

### Page 8

Although the contribution from individual projects is difficult to quantify, the surge in data centre
commencements during the second half of 2025 is likely supporting activity and should remain an
important source of construction demand over the second half of this year and beyond.

### Page 9

State detail
State outcomes were heavily influenced by the unwind of the mining-related spike in WA, masking
generally resilient conditions elsewhere. Excluding WA, construction activity was resilient, with Victoria
leading gains and the eastern states continuing to record gains.
Victoria (+5.8%qtr) recorded the strongest increase, while SA (+1.6%qtr), NSW (+1.4%qtr),
Queensland (+1.1%qtr) and the Northern Territory (+2.7%qtr) also posted gains.
In contrast, WA (-19.7%qtr) recorded a sharp decline as mining-related activity unwound following the

surge associated with the Scarborough Energy Project in the March quarter. Tasmania (-9.5%qtr) and
the ACT (-8.8%qtr) also recorded sizeable contractions.
Despite the quarterly volatility, construction activity remained higher than a year ago in most states,
with SA (+11.5%yr) recording the strongest annual growth, followed by Queensland (+7.9%yr), NSW
(+7.7%yr), WA (+7.7%yr) and Victoria (+6.7%yr). The exceptions were Tasmania (-4.3%yr), the ACT

(-15.7%yr) and the Northern Territory (-81.3%yr), where activity remained well below year-earlier levels.

Document 209: DeepSeek V4.1重回国产一哥！全新架构非对称Transformer，Engram用上了

Source PDF: all report pdf/DeepSeek V4.1重回国产一哥！全新架构非对称Transformer，Engram用上了.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 采⽤全新⾮对称架构，为 552B 参数 MoE 模型，输⼊、输出激活分别
为 8B 和 16B 。新模型⼤幅压缩 KV Cache ，使 HBM 需求降⾄ 1/4 、 SSD 降⾄ 1/8 。模型预训
练 45 万亿 token ，后训练重点转向数据与环境⼯程，并引⼊ Engram 条件记忆模块。
摘要：
DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 上线，跑分重回国产⼀哥！
⽽且这次，是完全重新训练的⾮对称模型新架构。
552B 参数的 MoE 模型，采⽤了全新的 Causal-Encoder-Decoder 结构，输⼊和输出不对称，输⼊
激活只有 8B ，输出激活 16B 。
这种架构成本显著低于已知的同尺⼨模型，难怪这两天测试感觉速度⻜快，原来不光是因为⽤
的⼈少。
这次重新预训练、⽤了新的数据、强化学习后训练规模更⼤，训练过程还与新版 DeepSeek
Harness v0.1.5 深度结合。
所以Flash 打败⾃家 Pro（除了知识容量），昨天还觉得离谱，今天⼀下⼦合理了起来。

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

V4.1 Flash 也是 DeepSeek ⾸个原⽣⽀持多模态视觉理解的正式版模型，此前视觉能⼒以实验版
V4 Flash Vision Exp 的形式单独提供，现在统⼀到了⼀个模型⾥。
再加上 9 ⽉ 14 ⽇起所有 V4 Pro 的请求会被重新路由到 V4.1 Flash 。
所以未来⼀段时间内，直到 V4.1 Pro 上线， DeepSeek API 将只提供这⼀个模型。
此外，还有⼀个能影响整个⾏业的消息。
推理效率⽅⾯， V4.1 Flash 的 KV Cache 缓存⼤⼩相⽐上⼀代模型，对 HBM 的需求减少到 1/4 ，
对 SSD 的需求减少到 1/8 。
从初代 V1 到 V4.1 Flash ， KV Cache ⾜⾜缩⼩到原来的 1/437 。

### Page 3

咱就是想问，内存啥时候降价？
51 ⻚论⽂解析
模型变化这么⼤，论⽂也少不了，刚放出的 pdf ⾜⾜有 51 ⻚。
从标题就在强调 “ 把 KV 缓存压缩到极致 ” 。
究竟怎么压缩这么狠的呢，先从架构开始说起。
⾮对称 Transformer ，提⽰词不必⾛完整 40 层
新架构是 40 层因果 Transformer ，被切分为前 20 层的因果编码器和后 20 层的解码器，这套结构命
名为 Causal Encoder-Decoder （ CED ）。
CED 受 YoCo 架构启发，但做了⼀系列结构改进来提升 KV Cache 容量和 KV ⽣成的计算深度。

### Page 4

CED 的核⼼在于⾮对称。编码器处理输⼊时激活 8B 参数，解码器⽣成输出时激活 16B 参数。
在 Agent ⼯作负载中，频繁的⼯具调⽤会产⽣⼤量 prefill 请求， CED 的处理⽅式是：解码器的全
局 KV Cache 不从各层⾃⾝的隐藏状态⽣成，⽽是直接从编码器最后⼀层（第 20 层）的隐藏状态
投射得到，每层使⽤独⽴的投射权重。
这样⼀来，⼤部分 prompt token 只需通过前 20 层编码器，不必再⾛完整的 40 层。
对于序列⻓度远⼤于滑动窗⼝的场景， prefill 计算复杂度直接从 O(NL) 降⾄约 O(NL/2) ，接近减
半。
CSA2 注意⼒：沿三个维度同时压缩缓存
接下来是注意⼒机制的改动。
Compressed Sparse Attention 2 （ CSA2 ）取代了 V4 中 CSA 与 HCA 的混合架构。
CSA2 沿三个维度同时压缩缓存：条⽬⼤⼩、序列维度和层维度。
跨层复⽤全局 KV 和 Top-K 索引来减少存储和计算。
CSA2 的压缩器和索引器也做了简化，去掉了 CSA 中相邻压缩条⽬之间的重叠和绝对位置嵌⼊，

索引器 K 改为从主 KV 条⽬投射获得，⽽⾮从隐藏状态单独压缩。
CSA2 定义了三种静态分配的⼯作模式。
Full 模式执⾏完整计算路径，⽣成⾃⼰的全局 KV ，投射索引器 K ，运⾏索引器产⽣ Top-K 索
引。
Reindex 模式复⽤前序 Full 层的全局 KV 和索引器 K ，但⽤⾃⼰的索引器查询重新评分，选出新
的 Top-K 。
Reuse 模式最轻量，直接复⽤前序层的全局 KV 和 Top-K 索引，不做任何索引计算。三种模式
下每层都保留⾃⼰的查询和滑动窗⼝ KV （ SWA KV ）。
三种模式下每层仍保留⾃⼰的查询和滑动窗⼝ KV 。编码器以少数纯 SWA 层开场，其余按组编
排，每组⼀个 Full 带若⼲ Reuse ；解码器则⼤量使⽤ Reindex 打头的组，在复⽤缓存的同时保留
逐层重新选择注意⼒⽬标的能⼒。

### Page 5

解码器另外引⼊层次化稀疏索引器。
第⼀个 Full 层在扫描全部可⻅位置的同时，通过逐块评分构建⼀个共享候选池；后续 Reindex 层
只在池内搜索。
由于池的⼤⼩固定，深层索引器的每查询计算量不再随上下⽂⻓度增⻓。这个机制是 training-
aware 的，在后训练中引⼊，训练与推理施加相同的候选约束。
缓存精度与持久化分层
缓存精度上， V4.1 Flash 将主 KV Cache 从 FP8 进⼀步压到 FP4，采⽤分组缩放因⼦的 E2M1 ⽅
案，并在论证数值范围安全后省略了⼆级全局缩放因⼦。
对量化更敏感的 SWA KV 仍保留 FP8 。 FP4 通过 QAT 在后训练阶段引⼊，量化点选在 RoPE 之后
—— 放在之前只有边际精度收益，却要付出解码开销。
部署侧引⼊SWA Bounded Replay 。
上⼀代精确重建 SWA KV 需要按层数成倍回放 token ，⽣产环境成本过⾼， V4.1 改为只回放最近
⼀个窗⼝的 token 做近似重建，对响应质量影响可忽略。

于是 KV Cache 被拆成两级， SWA KV 移出持久化层，改放在各机预留的⼀⼩部分主机 DRAM 组
成的分布式内存池中， TTL 只有分钟级，过期即回收；全局 KV 留在 SSD 上的持久化缓存，保证
数⼗⼩时⽣命周期。
其他架构扩展
- Single-Pass mHC：将输⼊混合系数偏移⼀个块，消除原始多核实现中的数据依赖；配合
Mega-mHC 部署内核，激活内存流量减半并触到理想下界。
- Engram 条件记忆模块：在浅层和中层各插⼊⼀处，多阶 n-gram 、多 hash head 索引，推理时
通过后台 RDMA 从主机内存预取嵌⼊。它贡献了模型中相当⼤⽐例的参数量。
- DSpark 投机解码：在⻣⼲冻结后独⽴训练，⽤少量 Transformer 块并⾏产出多个草稿位置的
logits ，配⼀个 Markov head 建模草稿 token 间依赖、⼀个置信度 head 预测逐位置接受概率，调度
器再结合引擎吞吐曲线为每个请求动态选择验证⻓度。

### Page 6

45 万亿 token 预训练，后训练只改数据不改算法
预训练，数据重新清洗
模型在数⼗万亿 token 的多模态语料上预训练，全程⽆不稳定。
稀疏注意⼒从零开始训练，没有任何密集注意⼒预热阶段；多模态数据从第⼀步就混⼊；⻓上
下⽂扩展在训练后段⼀次完成。
这次 DeepSeek 在数据侧提出两个判断。
⼀是把弱模型⽣成内容和低质量机器翻译定义为“隐性重复”并过滤掉，理由是它们主要是对已
有信息的改写，在⻓训练周期中可能有害。
⼆是发现爬⾍系统过度偏向纯⽂本⽹⻚，于是从 Common Crawl 重新引导抓取以提⾼多模态来源
覆盖。
交错图⽂数据的构建被组织成成本递增的流⽔线：在昂贵的图像检索之前先⽤启发式过滤、去
重和质量模型筛出⾼价值⽂档，组装成交错序列后再做图像感知的过滤与去重，最后交给 VLM
严格评分；被淘汰的⽂档还会部分回收为图⽂对。
优化器是分层的：线性变换权重⽤ Muon ，归⼀化与⾮矩阵参数⽤ AdamW ，嵌⼊表和预测头则
⽤基于 Sinkhorn 平衡的动量更新。交替对⾏列归⼀化以近似均衡更新矩阵的⾏列 RMS ，只需⼀

个动量缓冲区⽽⾮ Adam 的两个状态，直接压掉了巨量 Engram 参数带来的优化器状态内存。
Q/K 权重使⽤ head-wise Muon ，为不同注意⼒头提供不同预条件器。视觉编码器在学习率衰减
前保持冻结，仅归⼀化层与投射器可训练，衰减开始后再以更⼩学习率解冻联合优化。
后训练，只改数据不改算法
这次 V4.1 Flash 后训练流程沿⽤ SFT + RL + OPD 的标准范式，所有改变集中在数据与环境管
线。
原⽂写道： “ 在当前阶段，⼯程化数据和环境管线的边际回报远超后训练中算法创新的边际回
报。 ”
任务合成管线把每个训练任务形式化为（问题、环境、验证系统）三元组，沿难度与正确性两
个维度评估，再⽤这两个信号迭代训练 “ 造任务的模型 ” ，并在任务每次进⼊新的 RL 运⾏时⽤产⽣
的轨迹重新审计质量。
环境构建⾛了两条路。
General Agent 环境取材于内部员⼯和合作伙伴的真实交互数据与失败案例，据此重建 SaaS 、企
业应⽤和后端系统的 mock ⼯具集，连⼯具上下⽂、交互模式和失败条件⼀起复现。

Coding Agent 环境则由多个专⻔化 Agent 协作流⽔线产出。构建 Agent 判断项⽬可运⾏性与可验
证性并设计评测点，搭建 Agent 配置依赖、清除泄露解答的痕迹并打包镜像层，解题 Agent 尝试
完成任务，独⽴质检 Agent 审查事实错误、评测点匹配度和可攻击性⻛险，不通过则交修复
Agent 返⼯重验。

### Page 7

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
54 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
⽀撑规模的是⾃研沙箱平台DSec，训练期间并发实例达到百万级。它没有⽤ Kubernetes ，⽽是
⾃研放置引擎，以弱⼀致性换扩展性，多个副本不做同步协调，各⾃依据近期测量预测资源可
⽤性独⽴决策，准⼊约束由各节点本地执⾏。单物理节点通过 sub-NUMA 分区与 NUMA 绑定，把
容器密度提升了⼀倍以上，再配合延迟敏感执⾏类和超线程核⼼调度消除⼲扰。
RL 中出现了⼤量奖励⿊客和环境破坏⾏为。
Agent 利⽤了真实披露的内核驱动权限问题、安全模块的⾮法内存访问、从软件包镜像服务泄露
答案，也删过关键⼆进制、破坏系统⽂件、甚⾄移除整个⽂件系统。
防御⼿段是 per-sandbox 的 AppArmor 配置和基于 eBPF 的细粒度⽹络策略； Agent 弄崩环境会被

当作失败轨迹，并向 RL 框架回报⼀个  “repercussion” 信号。测试阶段同样出现类似⾏为。
Agent 会去反编译系统软件包挖漏洞。
可控推理强度
系统提⽰词中可加⼊⼀个标量 effort 级别， token 惩罚系数随其指数衰减， API 对外只暴露三档。
从低档到最⾼档，推理基准与 Agent 基准的成绩都有可观提升，代价是数倍输出 token ；但收益
并不线性，中⾼区间就能恢复最⼤设置的⼤部分准确率⽽ token 消耗不到⼀半，最后⼀段提升要
⽤近两倍的轨迹⻓度换边际改善。
虽然训练只⽤了有限⼏个级别，部署时中间值仍能引出插值后的推理⾏为。
OPD ：多教师与 token 级中断恢复
蒸馏阶段动⽤了四⼗余个教师模型，不同领域的最优教师可能来⾃模型开发的不同阶段，彼此
架构也未必相同。
配套的异步⽣成机制⽀持token 级中断与恢复： rollout 状态（含 KV Cache 与专家路由）以 token
粒度持久化，切换检查点后直接复⽤，彻底消除重新 prefill 的成本。

⼀句话总结
V4.1 Flash 的全部架构创新⼏乎都指向同⼀个⽬标，让 KV Cache 在层与层之间尽可能被复⽤、
在精度和存储层级上尽可能被压缩。
从⽽让⻓上下⽂ Agent 负载在经济上成⽴。
在后训练上， DeepSeek 公开承认算法已⾮瓶颈，⼏乎把资源全押在数据与环境⼯程。
本⽂来源：量⼦位

### Page 1

Financials - Taiwan

Insurance vs bank: Trading based on 
historical performance and tech exposure

Industry Overview

Insurance vs bank, investment vs interest, AI vs less AI 
Most investors in the Taiwan market favor tech/AI stocks, which account for >60% of 
total market cap in Taiwan, but we see rising interest in Taiwan financial stocks. In our 
view, there are two types of investors who trade Taiwan financial stocks: financial 
specialists who cover global or Asian financial sectors, and hedge fund investors who 
use financial stocks to balance their Taiwan portfolio or to hedge. The major Taiwan 
financial institutions we cover can be grouped based on their business mix: insurance-
centric, like Fubon and Cathay, and bank-centric like E.Sun. CTBC sits in the middle, as 
the revenue contribution from its banking and insurance businesses is similar, while 
Chailease is distinctive given its leasing business and Mainland China exposure. The 
insurance-centric FHCs are more exposed to market performance, as they invest in 
stocks, and investment income is the key driver of their earnings when changes in 
insurance underwriting income are not volatile. 
Preference in three different scenarios 
Though most Taiwan financial stocks reported strong earnings growth in the past 3-4 
years, when Taiwan’s macro economy and market were both booming, we have our 
preference in different scenarios. 
- Scenario 1: When tech/AI sectors rally, insurance-centric companies tend to outperform 
bank-centric companies but are likely to underperform the TWSE index and tech/AI leaders. 
The TWSE’s monthly performance was +7% or above in 8 of the past 32 months, and in 
these 8 months, Cathay outperformed E.Sun in all the periods while Fubon outperformed 
E.Sun in 5 months. However, Cathay and Fubon underperformed TWSE in 6 months.  
- Scenario 2: When tech/AI sectors are stable, insurance-centric companies tend to 
perform similarly to bank-centric companies and likely outperform the TWSE. The chance 
is almost 50/50, as some funds may seek more balanced exposure to different sectors. 
- Scenario 3: When tech/AI sectors drop, insurance-centric companies tend to 
underperform bank-centric companies, but likely outperform the TWSE. The TWSE’s 
monthly performance was negative in 10 of the past 32 months. In these 10months, 
Cathay/Fubon underperformed E.Sun in 6/7 months and outperformed TWSE in 6 months. 
Fund flows usually seek defensive stocks to hide in when tech/AI stocks correct. 
AI exposure in E.Sun/Fubon/Cathay 
It is difficult to have official estimates about AI exposure in different financial 
institutions, but we think our methods can give investors some angles to estimate. In 
short, we believe the AI impacts on different business lines, from big to small, are 
investment income > fee income, including wealth management fees and brokerage fees 
> interest income/asset quality. E.Sun’s exposure is <10% when short-term market 
correction occurs, while Fubon/Cathay could be at 25-35%.

### Page 2

How to invest in Taiwan financial stocks
Most investors in the Taiwan market favor tech/AI stocks, which account for >60% of
total market cap in Taiwan, but we see rising interest in Taiwan financial stocks. In our
view, there are two types of investors who trade Taiwan financial stocks: financial
specialists who cover global or Asian financial sectors, and hedge fund investors who use
financial stocks to balance their Taiwan portfolio or to hedge. The major Taiwan

financial institutions we cover can be grouped based on their business mix: insurance-
centric, like Fubon and Cathay, and bank-centric like E.Sun. CTBC sits in the middle, as
the revenue contribution from its banking and insurance businesses is similar, while
Chailease is distinctive given its leasing business and Mainland China exposure. The
insurance-centric FHCs are more exposed to market performance, as they invest in
stocks, and investment income is the key driver of their earnings when changes in

insurance underwriting income are not volatile.
Preference in three different scenarios
Though most Taiwan financial stocks reported strong earnings growth in the past 3-4
years, when Taiwan’s macro economy and market were both booming, we have our
preference in different scenarios.
- Scenario 1: When tech/AI sectors rally, insurance-centric companies tend to outperform
bank-centric companies but are likely to underperform the TWSE index and tech/AI leaders.

The TWSE’s monthly performance was +7% or above in 8 of the past 32 months, and in
these 8 months, Cathay outperformed E.Sun in all the periods while Fubon outperformed
E.Sun in 5 months. However, Cathay and Fubon underperformed TWSE in 6 months.
- Scenario 2: When tech/AI sectors are stable, insurance-centric companies tend to
perform similarly to bank-centric companies and likely outperform the TWSE. The chance
is almost 50/50, as some funds may seek more balanced exposure to different sectors.

- Scenario 3: When tech/AI sectors drop, insurance-centric companies tend to
underperform bank-centric companies, but likely outperform the TWSE. The TWSE’s
monthly performance was negative in 10 of the past 32 months. In these 10months,
Cathay/Fubon underperformed E.Sun in 6/7 months and outperformed TWSE in 6 months.
Fund flows usually seek defensive stocks to hide in when tech/AI stocks correct.
More data to show the shift between insurance and bank

In the past 32 months, from Jan 2024 to Aug 2026, the average monthly movement of
the TWSE (Taiwan’s main index, with a 42% contribution from TSMC and 79% from IT
companies) was at 3.1%. When the TWSE grew >3.1%/<3.1% in a month, if Fubon/Cathay
outperformed/underperformed E.Sun, we counted it as one hit; otherwise it was a miss.
The hit rate for Cathay/E.Sun was 74% and 58% for Fubon/E.Sun. In the past 13
consecutive months, the Cathay/E.Sun hit rate was at 100%, indicating that the shift

between insurance-centric and bank-centric along with tech stock movements has
already become market consensus.

BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

### Page 4

Business exposure to AI/tech stocks
It is difficult to have official estimates about AI exposure in different financial
institutions, but we think our methods can give investors some angles to estimate. In
short, we believe the AI impacts on different business lines, from big to small, are
investment income>fee income, including wealth management fees and brokerage
fees>interest income/asset quality.
E.Sun: Estimate <10% impact from market volatility

Before Sep 2026, when E.Sun consolidates the acquired E.Sun Life (previously Merc
Life), E.Sun had its major revenue/net profit from its banking business. In FY25 and
7M26, the net profit from banking accounted for 92% and 89% of the total, and the rest
was mainly from E.Sun securities which grew rapidly in the past 2-3 years as the
turnover in Taiwan market surged.
The ~10% of the net profit from non-banking could be impacted by market turnover
changes mainly. Unlike investment gains, turnover usually does not drop much even if

the market suffers from significant volatility, as in July. A long-time bear market could
hurt brokerage net profit, but we are not yet there.
In E.Sun’s banking revenue, NII (Net Interest Income), net fee income and other income
accounted for 47%, 31% and 12%. The main exposure to AI-related market volatility is in
net fee income as the acceleration/deceleration of NII growth is linked to the changes of
real economy instead of stock market and stock investment in other income is minimal.

In E.Sun Bank’s net fee income in FY25, the fees from mutual fund sales, and
bancassurance accounted for >70% of the total. The distribution of wealth management
products (mutual funds and bancassurance) is more impacted by market changes and
sentiment changes than other fee income.
With all the revenue sources being considered, we believe the AI-impacted part in E.Sun
is about 25% of its total revenue. And as the majority of this 25% is fee income instead

of investment income, the actual impact from short-term market volatility on E.Sun’s
revenue should be <10%, as fee income is more likely to decline but not disappear while
investment income could become investment losses.
Fubon & Cathay: IFRS 17 helps?
Market performance changes have more complicated impact on insurance-centric FHCs
as their insurance subsidiaries adopted IFRS 17 in 2026 with major accounting changes.
As FY25 and historical data before 2026 can no longer be used to gauge the impact, we

currently can only use 1H26 and some monthly data in 2026 to analyze.
Without taking FVOCI gains/losses into consideration, the net profit contribution from
Taipei Fubon Bank was at 26% of the total, while it was at 57% from Fubon Life when
investment income was impacted in Mar but later recovered in 2Q26. In a normalized
market, we expect the mix to be 30% and 60%.
Our methods to gauge Fubon’s exposure to AI/tech volatility are similar to those we use

for E.Sun. In Fubon’s banking business, net fee income accounted for ~25% of total
operating income. IFRS 17, in our view, does not make investment income and bottom
line of insurers less volatile. In Fubon’s life insurance revenue, investment related
income accounts for 50-75% of the operating income, depending on how bad/good the
investment results are. If net investment related income drops 50% in one quarter, the
impact on Fubon life’s operating income will be 25-37%.

We estimate the short-term market volatility impact on Fubon’s revenue at 25-35%, as
investment income could be vulnerable to deep and quick market correction.
Cathay is marginally more resilient than Fubon, as the contribution from Cathay United
Bank is at 35% of the total profit of Cathay, higher than Fubon, though bank fee income
accounts for 30% of the bank operation income, slightly higher than Fubon. However,
the overall impact is not very different between Fubon and Cathay in terms of their

sensitivity to tech/AI impacts.
